{"stdout": "__pycache__\nout_schema.py\ns_prompt.py\nu_prompt.py\n\"\"\"Schema for review_paper step — adversarial paper review.\n\nThree of the fields are gates rather than opinions. ``results_reported``\nasks whether the paper's numbers came out of an artifact that actually ran,\nbecause the cheapest way for a run to look finished is to write the numbers\nit expected; ``coverage`` asks whether the paper still answers the USER'S\noriginal request, because a run that narrows across iterations drifts off\nthat request one defensible step at a time; and ``blocking`` turns the two\nplus the soundness score into one boolean the loop can read without parsing\nprose. An audit of 19 finished runs found runs shipping a tiny\nexpected-direction effect as the answer with every score in the acceptable\nband — the review said nothing false, it simply had nowhere to say THIS IS\nNOT THE ANSWER TO WHAT WAS ASKED.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom typing import Annotated, Literal\n\nfrom aii_lib.prompts import LLMPrompt, LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOut, LLMStructOutModel\nfrom pydantic import Field\n\n\nclass Critique(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"A single actionable critique from the reviewer.\"\"\"\n\n    id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt] = Field(default=\"\", description=\"Critique ID (code-assigned)\")\n    category: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Category: 'methodology', 'evidence', 'novelty', 'clarity', 'scope', or 'rigor'\"\n    )\n    severity: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Severity: 'major' or 'minor'\"\n    )\n    description: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Clear description of the issue\"\n    )\n    suggested_action: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Concrete suggestion for how to address this critique\"\n    )\n\n\nclass DimensionScore(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Score for a single review dimension with improvement suggestions.\"\"\"\n\n    dimension: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Dimension name: 'soundness', 'presentation', or 'contribution'\"\n    )\n    score: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Score from 1 (poor) to 4 (excellent)\"\n    )\n    justification: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Brief justification for this score\"\n    )\n    improvements: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Specific improvements to raise the score (what + how + why)\",\n    )\n\n\nclass ReviewerFeedback(LLMPromptModel, LLMStructOutModel):\n    \"\"\"Adversarial review of the paper draft.\n\n    ID format: review_it{iteration}__{model}\n    \"\"\"\n\n    kind: Literal[\"reviewer_feedback\"] = \"reviewer_feedback\"\n    id: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt] = Field(default=\"\", description=\"Review ID (code-assigned)\")\n    overall_assessment: Annotated[str, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Overall assessment of the paper's quality and readiness\"\n    )\n    strengths: Annotated[list[str], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Key strengths of the paper\"\n    )\n    dimension_scores: Annotated[list[DimensionScore], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default_factory=list,\n        description=\"Scores (1-4) for: soundness, presentation, contribution\",\n    )\n    critiques: Annotated[list[Critique], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Actionable critiques — specific issues with concrete suggestions\"\n    )\n    results_reported: Annotated[bool, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=False,\n        description=(\n            \"True only when the paper's headline numbers come from an artifact that was \"\n            \"EXECUTED — a run that finished and wrote its output. False when any headline \"\n            \"number is projected, expected, illustrative, a placeholder, or produced by a \"\n            \"run that errored, was truncated, or never ran.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    coverage: Annotated[Literal[\"full\", \"partial\", \"lost\"], LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=\"partial\",\n        description=(\n            \"How much of the USER'S ORIGINAL request this paper answers: 'full' — it answers \"\n            \"the request; 'partial' — it answers a recognisable piece of it; 'lost' — the \"\n            \"paper answers a different question than the one asked.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    blocking: Annotated[bool, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=False,\n        description=(\n            \"True when this paper must not ship as it stands. Set it by rule, not by feel: \"\n            \"true when the soundness dimension score is 1 or lower, OR results_reported is \"\n            \"false, OR the headline claim contradicts the run's own evidence. Otherwise \"\n            \"false.\"\n        ),\n    )\n    score: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        description=\"Overall quality score from 1 (very strong reject) to 10 (award quality)\"\n    )\n    confidence: Annotated[int, LLMPrompt, LLMStructOut] = Field(\n        default=3,\n        description=\"Confidence in assessment from 1 (educated guess) to 5 (absolutely certain)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef format_critiques_for_prompt(feedback: ReviewerFeedback | dict) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Format reviewer critiques for inclusion in gen_strat/gen_paper_text prompts.\n\n    Accepts either a ReviewerFeedback object or a dict (from JSON resume).\n    \"\"\"\n    if isinstance(feedback, dict):\n        critiques = feedback.get(\"critiques\", [])\n        blocking = bool(feedback.get(\"blocking\", False))\n    else:\n        critiques = feedback.critiques\n        blocking = bool(feedback.blocking)\n\n    if not critiques:\n        return \"No critiques from previous review.\"\n\n    lines = []\n    # A blocking review is not more feedback to weigh against the rest — it is\n    # the reviewer saying the draft must not ship. This function is the ONE\n    # door every downstream prompt (gen_strat, gen_plan, gen_paper_text,\n    # upd_hypo) reads critiques through, so labelling here is what makes the\n    # next iteration treat them as requirements rather than suggestions.\n    if blocking:\n        lines.append(\n            \"The previous review is BLOCKING: the paper must not ship as it stands. \"\n            \"Every MUST-FIX item below is a requirement for this iteration, not a \"\n            \"suggestion — an iteration that leaves one unaddressed does not publish.\"\n        )\n        lines.append(\"\")\n    for c in critiques:\n        if isinstance(c, dict):\n            sev = c.get(\"severity\", \"minor\").upper()\n            cat = c.get(\"category\", \"\")\n            desc = c.get(\"description\", \"\")\n            action = c.get(\"suggested_action\", \"\")\n        else:\n            sev = c.severity.upper()\n            cat = c.category\n            desc = c.description\n            action = c.suggested_action\n        # Only the major items of a blocking review are must-fix; a blocking\n        # review's minor nits stay nits.\n        must_fix = \" MUST-FIX\" if blocking and sev == \"MAJOR\" else \"\"\n        lines.append(f\"- [{sev}{must_fix}] ({cat}) {desc}\")\n        lines.append(f\"  Action: {action}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\"\"\"System prompt for review_paper (Step 3.5: REVIEW_PAPER).\n\nExternal adversarial review — \"Fresh eyes on the paper.\"\nUses a DIFFERENT LLM than the one that wrote the paper (no creator bias).\nMirrors review_hypo style: review only, role in user prompt, web-grounded.\nScoring scales and review principles come from the shared review_rubric\ncomponent; paper-specific phrasing is passed as parameters.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.aii_context import get_aii_context\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.research_practices import PAPER_SECTIONS\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.review_rubric import get_review_rubric\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.tool_calling import get_web_tool_guidance\n\n_ASSESSMENT_DIMENSIONS = \"\"\"(a) Originality: Are the tasks or methods new? Novel combination of known techniques?\n    Clear differentiation from prior work? Is related work adequately cited?\n(b) Quality: Is the submission technically sound? Are claims well supported by theoretical\n    analysis or experimental results? Is the methodology appropriate? Is this a complete\n    piece of work? Are the authors honest about limitations?\n(c) Clarity: Is the submission clearly written and well organized? Does it provide enough\n    information for an expert to reproduce its results?\n(d) Significance: Are the results important? Would others build on them? Does it address\n    a meaningful problem better than prior work? Does it advance the state of the art?\"\"\"\n\n\ndef PROMPT(context: str) -> str:\n    rubric = get_review_rubric(\n        assessment_dimensions=_ASSESSMENT_DIMENSIONS,\n        soundness_desc=(\n            \"soundness of the technical claims, experimental and research methodology,\\n\"\n            \"and whether central claims are adequately supported with evidence\"\n        ),\n        contribution_desc=(\n            \"quality of the overall contribution, importance of questions asked,\\n\"\n            \"originality of ideas and execution, value to the broader research community\"\n        ),\n        major_issue_meaning=\"would cause rejection\",\n        extra_principles=(\n            \"- Check the STRUCTURE against what an expert in the field expects:\"\n            f\" {PAPER_SECTIONS}. Flag a literature survey or method detail sitting in\"\n            \" the Introduction, and any standard section missing although the paper\"\n            \" has the content for it, as a major clarity issue — not a nit\",\n            \"- Check the paper is readable RESULTS-FIRST: key numbers stated in the\"\n            \" abstract, in the contributions list and at the opening of Results; a main\"\n            \" results table comparing the method against its baselines; at least one\"\n            \" results figure per major claim; every figure and table interpreted in the\"\n            \" text. Results prose with no numbers in it, a missing main table, or a\"\n            \" claim no figure supports are each a major issue\",\n            \"- Check figure PLACEMENT, TYPE and COUNT: each figure sitting in the section\"\n            \" that discusses it (hero in the Introduction, diagrams in Method, results\"\n            \" figures in Results, ablations in Results or Discussion, none in the\"\n            \" Abstract, Related Work or Conclusion), a chart type that matches the data\"\n            \" relationship, roughly four to eight figures with the main results figure\"\n            \" first, and captions that stand on their own\",\n            \"- Check if figures are well-specified and would effectively communicate the results\",\n            \"- Verify that claims are supported by the artifacts described\",\n            \"- Screen for unattributed reuse. Search the web for the paper's distinctive\"\n            \" phrasings, its central claim, and any method name it coins. If wording,\"\n            \" a derivation, or a result appears in prior work, say so and name the source.\"\n            \" Treat close paraphrase of a source's argument without citation the same as\"\n            \" verbatim reuse\",\n            \"- Check that any prior work the paper builds on is cited at the point it is\"\n            \" used, not only in a related-work list. An uncited source that the work\"\n            \" depends on is a major issue, not a presentation nit\",\n            \"- Check the cited sources exist and say what they are claimed to say. Flag any\"\n            \" reference you cannot verify, and any retracted or predatory-venue source\",\n            \"- Check that every headline number came out of an artifact that ACTUALLY RAN.\"\n            \" A projected, expected, illustrative or placeholder number presented as a\"\n            \" result is the most serious defect a paper can have, whatever its prose\"\n            \" quality — set results_reported false and blocking true\",\n            \"- Check COVERAGE against the user's ORIGINAL request, not against the paper's\"\n            \" own framing. A paper that answers a question adjacent to the one that was\"\n            \" asked is not a small scope issue; say which part of the request went\"\n            \" unanswered\",\n            \"- Check that the headline claim is PROPORTIONATE to the evidence and to what\"\n            \" the original request implied. A small, expected-direction effect written up\"\n            \" as the answer is the failure mode to name explicitly: either the paper\"\n            \" states why that effect is itself the answer, or the claim overreaches\",\n        ),\n    )\n    return f\"\"\"{context}\n\nROLE: You are a very experienced and critical conference reviewer.\nYour expertise spans the domain of the paper under review.\nYou have served on program committees at top-tier venues in the relevant field.\n\nTASK: Perform a deep and honest review (at the level of a top-tier venue submission) of the paper.\n\nFIGURES: The paper contains figure specifications with captions and descriptions but the\nactual images have not been generated yet. Assume each figure shows exactly what its\ncaption describes — do not penalize for missing images.\n\nARTIFACTS: The paper references code artifacts via [ARTIFACT:id] markers. The correct\nURLs to the artifact folders will be added later — do not penalize for missing links.\n\nGOAL: Your review feeds directly back to the paper author. The objective is to maximize\nthe overall review score in subsequent rounds. Every piece of feedback you give should\nbe written with this goal in mind — prioritize the critiques and suggestions that would\nproduce the largest score improvement if addressed. Don't waste the author's iteration\nbudget on low-impact polish when there are score-blocking issues to fix.\n\n{rubric}\n\n{get_web_tool_guidance()}\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Get system prompt for adversarial paper review.\"\"\"\n    return PROMPT(context=get_aii_context(focus=\"review_paper\"))\n\"\"\"User prompt for review_paper (Step 3.5: REVIEW_PAPER).\n\nAdversarial review of the paper draft by a different LLM.\nMirrors review_hypo style: role in user prompt, web-grounded, scores (a)-(e).\nOutput: structured feedback with actionable critiques.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom typing import TYPE_CHECKING\n\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.domain_handbooks import get_domain_handbooks_section\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.research_practices import PAPER_SECTIONS\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.review_rubric import REVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.user_folder import get_user_folder_prompt\nfrom aii_pipeline.prompts.components.user_request import get_user_request_prompt\n\nif TYPE_CHECKING:\n    from aii_pipeline.prompts.steps._3_invention_loop._3_gen_art.out_schema import (\n        BaseArtifact,\n    )\n\n\n_ART_FIELDS: set[str] = {\n    \"id\",\n    \"type\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"summary\",\n    \"workspace_path\",\n    \"out_expected_files\",\n}\n\n\ndef PROMPT(\n    paper_text: str,\n    artifacts_str: str,\n    previous_critiques_text: str,\n) -> str:\n    return f\"\"\"{REVIEWER_ROLE_BLOCK}\n\n<paper>\n{paper_text}\n</paper>\n\n{\n        f'''<supplementary_materials>\nThe authors' code, data, and experimental artifacts. You may read these to verify\nclaims made in the paper — check if the code matches the described methodology,\nif the results are reproducible, and if the data supports the conclusions.\n\n{artifacts_str}\n</supplementary_materials>'''\n        if artifacts_str\n        else \"\"\n    }\n\n{\n        get_domain_handbooks_section(\n            focus=\"judging whether the paper's contribution is genuinely novel versus already-done or a known dead end in this field\"\n        )\n    }\n\n{\n        f'''<previous_review>\nYour review from the previous iteration. Check which critiques have been addressed\nin the revised paper. Do NOT re-raise critiques that have been adequately fixed.\nOnly re-raise if the fix is insufficient.\n\n{previous_critiques_text}\n</previous_review>'''\n        if previous_critiques_text\n        else \"\"\n    }\n\n<task>\nReview this paper as you would for a top-tier venue submission.\n\nSTEP 1 — READ THE PAPER: Read it carefully. Note claims, methodology, and results.\n\nSTEP 2 — CHECK THE CODE: Read the supplementary materials to verify the paper's claims.\nDo the experiments match what's described? Are there discrepancies between code and paper?\n\nSTEP 3 — SEARCH THE LITERATURE: Ground your review in evidence.\n- Search for the closest existing work — is this genuinely novel or incremental?\n- Check if the proposed methodology has known failure modes\n- What level of contribution gets accepted at top venues in this area?\n\nSTEP 4 — CHECK COVERAGE AGAINST THE ORIGINAL REQUEST: The user's original request that\nstarted this run is supplied as a separate message in this turn. Read it and ask what it\nactually asked for. Does this paper answer THAT, or a question next to it? Set `coverage`\nto \"full\", \"partial\" or \"lost\", and when it is not \"full\", raise a critique naming the\npart of the request that went unanswered. Judge against the request, not against the\npaper's own framing of it — a run that narrows one defensible step per iteration ends up\nanswering something nobody asked, and each step looked fine on its own.\n\nSTEP 5 — CHECK THE STRUCTURE, THE RESULTS AND THE HEADLINE CLAIM:\n- Does the paper run the sections an expert expects — {PAPER_SECTIONS}? Raise a major\n  clarity critique for a literature survey or method detail left in the Introduction, and\n  for a standard section the paper has the content for but never gives its own heading.\n- Can a reader get the main finding from the abstract, the main results table and the first\n  results figure alone? Raise a critique for Results prose with no numbers in it, a missing\n  main results table comparing the method against its baselines, a major claim with no\n  figure behind it, or a figure or table the text never interprets.\n- Is each figure where a reader needs it — hero diagram at the end of the Introduction,\n  diagrams in Method, results figures in Results, ablations in Results or Discussion, and\n  none in the Abstract, Related Work or Conclusion — with a chart type that fits the data\n  relationship, a sensible count (roughly four to eight), and a self-contained caption?\n- Are the headline numbers from an artifact that ACTUALLY RAN? Trace each one to an\n  executed output in the supplementary materials. A projected, expected, illustrative or\n  placeholder number presented as a result means `results_reported` is false.\n- Is the headline claim PROPORTIONATE? A tiny effect, or an effect in the direction\n  everyone already expected, dressed up as the answer is not a presentation nit — either\n  the paper states why that effect is itself the answer (a bound someone needed, a belief\n  it overturns, a mechanism only visible at that size), or the claim overreaches and you\n  say so.\n- Does the headline claim CONTRADICT the run's own evidence anywhere — a table, a figure,\n  a log, an artifact summary? Name the contradiction.\n- Set `blocking` by rule: true when the soundness score is 1 or lower, OR\n  `results_reported` is false, OR the headline claim contradicts the run's own evidence.\n\nSTEP 6 — WRITE YOUR REVIEW:\nFor each critique:\n1. Categorize: methodology, evidence, novelty, clarity, scope, or rigor\n2. Rate severity: major (would cause rejection) or minor (polish)\n3. Describe the issue clearly\n4. Suggest a concrete action to address it\n\nFocus on the most impactful issues. Provide your review via structured output.\n</task>\"\"\"\n\n\ndef get(\n    paper_text: str,\n    artifacts: list[BaseArtifact],\n    previous_critiques_text: str | None = None,\n    user_folder_path: str = \"\",\n) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Build user prompt for adversarial paper review.\"\"\"\n    from aii_lib.prompts import LLMPromptModel\n\n    prompt = PROMPT(\n        paper_text=paper_text or \"No paper text available.\",\n        artifacts_str=LLMPromptModel.list_to_prompt_yaml(\n            artifacts,\n            label=\"Item\",\n            include=_ART_FIELDS,\n            strip_nulls=True,\n        )\n        or \"\",\n        previous_critiques_text=previous_critiques_text or \"\",\n    )\n    return prompt + get_user_folder_prompt(user_folder_path) + get_user_request_prompt()", "stderr": "\nShell cwd was reset to /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_YqmEFECOIR3D/3_invention_loop/iter_3/review_paper/review_paper", "interrupted": false, "isImage": false, "noOutputExpected": false}

Paper §3.1 (line 48): *"Lane~C holds out 10 checkpoints not seen during Lane~A analysis, drawn from four families (Qwen3, SmolLM3, Phi-4-mini, Granite-3.2), including both parent and abliterated pairs."*

- B3: ρ=-0.890, family CI [-0.951,-0.569] — MATCH.
- C13 (peak d): ρ=-0.656, family CI [-0.975,0.200] — MATCH.
- BL1: ρ=-0.620, family CI [-0.735,0.200] — MATCH.
- BL1_hard: ρ=-0.534, family CI [-0.705,0.413] — MATCH.

CIs are the **family-bootstrap** column (README calls this "Family 95% CI"; there is also a separate "checkpoint-bootstrap" CI column — paper only uses family CIs, consistent with its stated method). 

**Δ vs BL1 sign issue:** paper shows B3 Δ=-0.270, C13 Δ=-0.037. The artifact's own `|ρ|−|ρ(BL1)|` paired quantity is **+0.270** (CI [-0.240,0.695]) for B3 and **+0.037** (CI [-0.543,0.650]) for C13 — the paper has silently negated this column (reporting signed ρ_readout − ρ_BL1 instead of the artifact's magnitude-excess quantity), and **prints no CI at all for the Δ column**, so a reader cannot see that it "does not exclude zero" from the table itself — that claim is only true by cross-referencing the artifact's paired CI (which does span zero either way).

**"six activation readouts" claim — MISMATCH.** Paper text at line 178: *"Table~\ref{tab:heldout} reports Spearman correlations... for the six activation readouts and BL1."* Table 4 lists only 2 substantive rows (B3, C13_peak_d) plus 2 BL1 variants — not six, and not the six readouts Lane A defines (held-out d, R, peak drive, onset depth, k-AUROC, B3). The actual Lane C prereg registered **17 readouts** (C1–C13 with several sub-variants, BL1, BL1_hard, B3, B7, X2, X10_abs — `prereg.json: operational_definitions` keys C1–C14, BL1, B3, B7, W_parent_free). All their rhos (from `results/heldout_table.json`):

| feature | ρ(HC) | feature | ρ(HC) |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | -0.215 | C10 | -0.423 |
| C1_k4 | -0.301 | C10_screenrule | -0.068 |
| C1_k8 | -0.313 | C11 | -0.006 |
| C2 | **0.656** | C12 | -0.239 |
| C2_tpr5 | **0.767** | C12_topsv | 0.006 |
| C2_screenrule | **0.767** | C12_screenrule | -0.227 |
| C3 | **0.620** | C13 | -0.252 |
| C3_screenrule | 0.374 | C13_peak_d | -0.656 |
| C4 | 0.381 | C13_peak_f | 0.019 |
| C4_screenrule | 0.063 | BL1 | -0.620 |
| C5 | -0.620 | BL1_hard | -0.534 |

| C7 | -0.620 | X2 | 0.313 |

**Omissions confirmed real:** B7 (ρ=0.804, weight-only, the single **strongest** |ρ| on the whole panel, stronger than B3) is entirely absent from the paper — I grepped the full .tex and it never appears. Likewise C2 (0.656), C2_tpr5 (0.767), C2_screenrule (0.767), C3 (0.620), C6 (0.693), C9 (0.644) — the six sign-reversed candidates, several of which clear the critical |ρ|=0.648 — are never mentioned. C5, C7 (both ρ=-0.620, same magnitude as BL1) are also absent from the paper body. The paper reports only the two candidates most favorable to its "no readout beats BL1" narrative and silently drops the ones with the largest and most surprising deviations (B7, C2/C6/C9 sign-flip).

## 3. Two new abliterated pairs replicate — MATCH

README/`extra_analyses` paired-contrast table:
- Vikhr peak-d Δ = -0.904 [-1.384, -0.550] → paper's "-0.90 [-1.38, -0.55]" — MATCH.
- Llama(mylesgoose abl2) peak-d Δ = -0.606 [-0.883, -0.358] → paper's "-0.61 [-0.88, -0.36]" — MATCH.

Note: the paper text itself (line 197) only states this qualitatively ("held-out d drops in both, CIs below zero") and never prints these numeric values in the .tex body — the numeric values you supplied match the artifact but do not actually appear anywhere in `paper_draft.tex`. Grep confirms no "Vikhr"/"Falcon"/"AMD"/"LFM2"/"OLMo" string exists anywhere in the paper.

## 4. Omitted findings — CONFIRMED, all omitted, none disclosed

Grepped the full paper for "AMD", "OLMo", "Vikhr", "Falcon", "LFM2", "double norm", "BL1_truelogit", "0.648", "0.797", "MDE", "critical" — **none of these appear** except one coincidental unrelated "critical" (Lane B discussion) and one coincidental "0.767" value (a different quantity — Held-out-d's ρ-with-BL1 in the *25-checkpoint Lane A* table, Table 5, not the Lane C panel).

Specifically omitted from the paper, all present in the artifact:
- **BL1 +1.61 on AMD SFT→DPO no-op** (`ΔHC -0.022 [-0.156,0.111]`, `ΔBL1 1.614 [0.983,2.285]`, `Δpeak_d 0.058 [-0.075,0.159]`) — this is arguably the paper's own headline claim ("no activation readout beats BL1") turned on its head on a single fresh pair: BL1 moves sharply on a behavioural no-op while activation readouts (peak d, C7) do not. Completely unreported.
- **AMD base→SFT** contrast (ΔHC 0.556, ΔBL1 0.345, ΔC13 0.068) — unreported.
- **BL1 double-norm bug** (`bl1_double_norm_final_slice` deviation): BL1 is actually computed as `norm(norm(h_L))` (the iteration-2 lens re-applies final-norm to an already-post-norm `hidden_states[-1]`), not the model's literal final logits. `BL1_truelogit` is reported beside it (ρ=-0.620 held-out, identical rank) — the paper's notation table (Table 3) defines BL1 as "the logit difference between the top refusal token and the top compliance token at the last prompt position in the final layer," which is the *intended* definition but not what the code computes; this discrepancy and its resolution (`hook_checks.json`, rank-correlation=1.00) are never mentioned.
- **Per-prompt Vikhr recognition vs drive** (0.850→0.812 recognition, 0.787→0.369 drive under abliteration) — matches your reported .85→.81 / .79→.37; unreported in the paper.
- **Critical value 0.648 / MDE 0.797 at n=10** — never stated in the paper; the reader has no way to judge from the .tex alone how weak the n=10 design is statistically (the artifact leads with this number).

## 5. Prompt set / HC values — panel MATCH, source description PARTIAL

Paper §3.2: *"45 harmful prompts from XSTest and JailbreakBench, each paired with a benign twin."* Artifact: Lane C = 90 items (45 harmful + 45 benign), `assets/behaviour_items.json` item categories include both XSTest-style tags (`historical_events`, `homonyms`, `figurative_language`, `safe_contexts`) and JailbreakBench-style harm categories (`Non-violent crimes`, `Hate, harassment and discrimination`, `Sexual content`) — consistent with a mixed XSTest+JailbreakBench source (MATCH on composition, count matches: 45+45=90). This artifact itself contains no `JailbreakBench` string (the mixture is inherited from iteration-2's Lane C construction, which I did not independently open — outside this artifact's scope).

The `items_cut_to_laneC90` deviation (§7 below) is real and **is disclosed in the artifact but not in the paper**: from checkpoint 2 onward only the 90 Lane C items were generated/judged (the reserved 54-item XSTest split, 108 more items, was never run for 9/10 checkpoints due to CPU-contention time pressure). The paper's "45 harmful prompts" description happens to match the *actual* primary-outcome item set used (by luck/design, since the deviation makes the Lane C 90 the operative definition), but the paper gives no indication that a larger intended item set (198) was truncated mid-panel.

## 6. B3 definition — MISMATCH (significant)

Paper Table 3 (`tab:notation`): *"$B3$ — Direction self-consistency: Fisher-transformed mean cosine similarity of per-layer diff-in-means directions across consecutive layers."*

Actual code (`prereg.json: operational_definitions.B3`, `src/candidates.py:44`, `src/h2/score_ckpt.py:315-317`):
```
B3 = "BL3_DIFFMEAN exactly as iteration 2 (held-out Cohen's d of the cross-fitted
      diff-in-means projection at the checkpoint's own l_star, EASY)."
out["BL3_DIFFMEAN"] = cohens_d(s_dm[ok & y==1], s_dm[ok & y==0])
```
B3 is a **Cohen's-d separability statistic at a single best layer** (structurally the same family of quantity as held-out d / C13), computed via `crossfit_diffmeans_scores`. There is no cosine similarity, no consecutive-layer comparison, and no Fisher z-transform anywhere in B3's computation. Grepping the whole `src/` tree for "Fisher"/"consecutive"/"self_consist" finds nothing matching this description; the closest actual "self-consistency" concept in the codebase is **C3** (`|cos(u_shallow, u_deep)|`, a two-point shallow-vs-deep cosine, ρ=0.620, wrong-signed) — a different candidate entirely. This means the paper's headline held-out result (B3, ρ=-0.89, "direction self-consistency achieves the strongest correlation") **mischaracterizes what B3 actually measures**. It is not a novel geometric-consistency readout; it is essentially a repackaged separability/Cohen's-d metric, closely related to (and correlated with) the family of readouts (C1, C13, held-out d) the paper already discusses elsewhere as capturing separability.

**Sign check:** higher B3 ↔ lower HC is confirmed both by the registered sign (`expected_sign_vs_harmful_compliance: B3 = -1`) and the observed ρ=-0.890, so paper's directional claim (B3 "correlates most strongly," implicitly negative direction consistent with more separable models being safer) is directionally fine — only the *definition* is wrong.

## 7. All deviations recorded vs. disclosed in paper

17 deviations in `results/deviations.json`. Paper discloses **essentially none** of them explicitly (no deviations/limitations section references any artifact-specific execution deviation by name). Cross-check:

| deviation key | disclosed in paper? |
|---|---|
| cpu_only_box | No |
| gen_batch_32 | No |
| greedy_shrink_decoder | No |
| generation_order_and_late_member_rule | No |
| items_cut_to_laneC90 | No (paper's "45 harmful prompts" reads as an intentional design, not a truncation) |
| no_S_U | No |
| no_D_resp | No |
| lfm2_arch_patches | No |
| template_borrowed (AMD-OLMo-1B-SFT chat template) | No |
| judge_primary_is_laneC_protocol (plan asked for stance-framed judge; not available, substituted) | No |
| session2_resume_harvest (venv reaped, harvest resumed) | No |
| peak_rss_cpu (RAM guard exceeded, no crash) | No |
| post_prereg_additions (extra analyses added after prereg freeze) | No — paper presents leave-one-family-out, BL1_truelogit, etc. as if part of the design without flagging they're post-hoc/descriptive, not pre-registered |
| bl1_double_norm_final_slice | No (see item 4/6) |
| b7_architecture_null | No (and B7 itself is omitted, see item 2) |
| hf_cache_removed_for_deployment | No (irrelevant to paper) |

The Limitations section (§5.4) does mention some *general* study-design limitations (architecture scope, prompt-only d, no causal intervention, teacher-forcing, saturation, gate failures, "commissioned metric not delivered") but **none of the 17 artifact-level execution deviations** listed above are surfaced, including the two most consequential for interpreting Table 4: `items_cut_to_laneC90` (which item set actually backs "primary outcome") and `b7_architecture_null` (which explains why the panel's single strongest correlate is an artifact, and why B7 is silently dropped from the table rather than shown-and-caveated).

## 8. Preregistration / hash chain and screen→join — CONFIRMED

- Hash chain (`hash_chain.jsonl`, `results/order_audit.json`): panel_rule → panel draw → panel_trim → **graded_truth.json committed 13:16:45.48Z** → **prereg.json frozen 13:16:46.56Z** (~1s later, truth-before-prereg). `order_audit.json: "truth_before_prereg": true`, `"pass": true`; every one of the 10 checkpoints' harvest files postdate the prereg commit timestamp (`after_prereg: true`, gate=PASS for all 10). So yes — held-out panel behavior was graded and hash-committed **before** the scoring rules (prereg) were frozen, and before any activation hook ran on any panel checkpoint. This is a legitimately strong pre-registration design.
- Screen→join: **DEFERRED**, confirmed. `results/join_stub.json`: `"screen_survivor_found": false`; README: *"no screen `survivor.json` exists under `iter_3/gen_art/*/`... this iteration's evaluation artifact rebuilt the three-model tables and ran no C1-C14 screen."* The paper does not mention this deferred-join status at all — it presents Table 4 as if it is the complete confirmatory test, without noting that the formal screen→held-out confirmation criterion (`join_stub.json`'s sign-and-exceeds-BL1 rule) was never actually applied because no screen survivor file exists to join against.

---

### Summary of most serious issues for the paper authors/reviewers
1. **Families/checkpoints in §3.1 are fabricated/wrong** — directly contradicted by the artifact's own panel-selection log (item 1).
2. **B3's definition in the notation table does not match its code** — the headline held-out finding is mischaracterized as a novel "direction self-consistency" (cosine-based) metric when it is actually a Cohen's-d separability statistic at a single layer (item 6).
3. **Table 4 cherry-picks 2 of 17 preregistered readouts**, omitting B7 (strongest correlate, later shown to be an architecture artifact) and the six sign-reversed candidates (C2/C2_tpr5/C2_screenrule/C3/C6/C9), materially changing the picture from "nothing beats BL1" to "several readouts numerically exceed the critical threshold, several with the wrong sign, and the strongest of all is a confound" (item 2, 4, 7).
4. **17 real execution deviations are disclosed in the artifact but none in the paper**, including a truncated item set for 9/10 checkpoints and a double-normalization bug in the baseline metric BL1 itself (item 4, 6, 7).
5. Power context (critical ρ=0.648, MDE=0.797) that the artifact leads with is absent from the paper, making the n=10 CIs harder for a reader to judge (item 2, 4).

These are concrete, artifact-grounded discrepancies suitable for blocking/major-revision review comments, keyed to the file+line citations above (mainly `paper_draft.tex` lines 48, 178, 197, Table 3/tab:notation, Table 4/tab:heldout, vs. `README.md`, `prereg.json`, `results/heldout_table.json`, `results/deviations.json`, `results/order_audit.json`, `results/join_stub.json`, `src/candidates.py`, `src/h2/score_ckpt.py`).

Here are the details:\n\n\"Where Do Reasoning Models Refuse?\" is authored by Kureha Yamaguchi from The Alan Turing Institute, Benjamin Etheridge from University of Oxford, and Andy Arditi from Northeastern University.\n\n**Summary of the paper:**\n\nChat models without chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning must decide whether to refuse a harmful request before generating their first response token. Reasoning models, by contrast, produce extended chains of thought before their final output, raising a natural question: where in this process does the decision to refuse occur?\n\n**Key findings:**\n\n- The CoT causally influences refusal outcomes; fixing a specific reasoning trace substantially reduces variance in whether the model ultimately refuses or complies.\n\n- In distilled models, subtle differences in the opening sentence of the CoT can fully determine the model's refusal decision, and these patterns transfer across models distilled from the same teacher.\n\n- Directional ablation increases harmful compliance across all four reasoning models.\n\nThe paper is available on arXiv with identifier 2507.03167."], "durationSeconds": 6.047242090000014, "searchCount": 1}

It withdraws the accumulator and the site-level 'execution' reading. It adds the abliterated row (d 2.45 -> 0.71, delta -1.73 [-2.35, -1.19]) and admits the few-prompt metric is not delivered. I traced the core numbers to executed artifacts. Table 1 (HC, d, R, drive, onset), Table 3 (site deltas), Table 5 (bl1_corr) and the printed cells of Table 4 all reproduce from gen_art_evaluation_1/results/tables.json and gen_art_experiment_1/results/heldout_table.json. The held-out panel was graded and hash-committed before prereg froze (order_audit truth_before_prereg=true). results_reported is therefore TRUE.

The paper is still not shippable, for four verified reasons.

(1) THE HELD-OUT PANEL IS DESCRIBED AS A DIFFERENT PANEL. Sec. 3.1 says Lane C draws '10 checkpoints ... from four families (Qwen3, SmolLM3, Phi-4-mini, Granite-3.2)'. The actual panel is Vikhr-Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct plus its abliterated child, Falcon3-1B Base/Instruct, AMD-OLMo-1B base/SFT/SFT-DPO, Llama-3.2-1B-Instruct plus mylesgoose abliterated2, and LFM2-700M. Those are the llama3.2, falcon3, amd-olmo and lfm2 families. The artifact README states that the four families the paper names were EXCLUDED because they were in the screen panel. The 'held-out' claim is the paper's only cross-family evidence, and as written it describes a leaked, non-held-out panel.

(2) THE HEADLINE HELD-OUT READOUT IS MIS-DEFINED, AND 'B3' IS TWO DIFFERENT QUANTITIES. The notation table defines B3 as 'direction self-consistency: Fisher-transformed mean cosine of consecutive-layer diff-in-means directions'. No such computation exists in either artifact. In the held-out panel, B3 = BL3_DIFFMEAN, the held-out Cohen's d of the cross-fitted diff-in-means projection at the checkpoint's own l_star (src/candidates.py:44; h2/score_ckpt.py). In Lane A / Table 1 / Table 5, B3 = B3_fisher = BL4_CLUSTSEP, a Fisher cluster-separation ratio (eval.py:263; src/notation.py:33). That is why Table 1 shows B3 = 28.1 and 33.2, which is impossible for a Fisher-z of a cosine. So the paper's strongest result (rho = -0.89) is a separability statistic, the same family as d and C13, and not a cross-layer geometry readout. The 'self-consistency' framing also sits in direct tension with Contribution 2's claim that the direction is layer-specific.

(3) TABLE 4 REPORTS 2 OF THE 30+ REGISTERED HELD-OUT ROWS, AND THE OMITTED ROWS CHANGE THE STORY. The text says the table has 'the six activation readouts'. It prints B3, C13_peak_d and two BL1 variants. The omitted rows are: B7 (weights-only, rho = +0.804, the second-largest |rho| on the panel, shown by b7_diagnostic to be an OLMo all-ones-null artefact); C2/C2_tpr5/C2_screenrule (+0.66 to +0.77), C6 (+0.69) and C9 (+0.64), which all clear the critical |rho| = 0.648 with the OPPOSITE sign to their pre-registration; and C5/C7 (-0.62, tied with BL1). The critical value 0.648 and the MDE 0.797 at n = 10 never appear. The Delta-vs-BL1 column is sign-flipped relative to the artifact (|rho|-|rho_BL1| = +0.270, CI [-0.240, 0.695]) and prints no CI. The most mechanistically interesting held-out result is also absent. On the AMD SFT->SFT-DPO behavioural no-op (dHC -0.022), BL1 moves +1.61 [0.98, 2.29] while peak d (+0.058 [-0.075, 0.159]) and C7 do not. That replicates the Granite null-edit specificity on a fresh family and is exactly the evidence that an activation readout adds something the logit gap does not.

(4) THE LAYER-SPECIFIC-DIRECTION CLAIM (CONTRIBUTION 2) IS ESTABLISHED ONLY IN-DISTRIBUTION, AND LANE B IS MIS-DESCRIBED. The evaluation artifact's own deviation says the HARD-prompt fixed-axis test is UNDEFINED in every 4B arm, because the EASY-fit layer-13 axis has a negative HARD start gap. The reported Spearman values (-0.99/-0.82/-0.87/-0.97/-0.88) are the added 'EASY cross-fitted, in-distribution' variant, and the paper never says so. On HARD prompts the fixed-axis Spearman is +0.31 (Base), +0.63 (STaR) and +0.15 (Abliterated), so 'decays in every arm' does not hold on held-out prompts. The HARD own-axis ratio of 19.7 is quoted as supporting evidence, but it is really a sign that the in-distribution and held-out pictures disagree. 'This pattern holds identically in all four Lane B lineages across all five dose levels and all three activation sites' has no artifact behind it. Lane B is also mis-described. The artifact inventory gives L1 = Qwen3-4B-BASE, L2 = Instruct, L3 = SafeRL and L4 = STaR, each with its own r_ablit fit layer (29/22/23/29). The paper says L1 = Instruct and L2 = 'Instruct, different random seed for the direction', and then calls L1-L3 'the safety-tuned lineages' in the key L4 contrast. L1 is the base model, which barely refuses (HC 0.022 but incoherent), so the safety-vs-non-safety contrast set is itself mislabelled.

Beyond these, the paper leaves 13+ of the artifacts' execution deviations undisclosed (items_cut_to_laneC90, BL1 double-norm, post_prereg_additions, judge substitution). It does not state which BL1 variant each table uses: Table 1 is BL1_hard, the STaR 46% figure is BL1_easy, and the two differ in sign for the abliterated model (-1.96 vs +1.76). Two cited keys (Orgad2026, Yamaguchi2025) are missing from references.bib, and 15 bib entries are never cited. The contributions are all honest nulls or scoped replications. The abliteration d-drop replicates Llorente-Saguer 2026a/b, and recognition-vs-execution is prior art (Orgad, Lin). The one piece that is new and matches the user's 'where safety lives' mech-interp framing is the specificity result: held-out request-axis d drops 6/6 plus 2/2 fresh pairs and does NOT move on either behavioural no-op (Granite +0.09, AMD SFT->DPO +0.06), while BL1 and peak drive move on both. That result is underplayed and partly omitted. I recommend reject in this round, with a short and concrete path: fix the panel description, B3's definition and naming, the Lane B lineage labels and the HARD-vs-EASY accumulator caveat; print the full held-out table; and lead with the null-edit specificity dissociation."""

# Bibliography Verification Report — run_YqmEFECOIR3D iter_3 paper_draft.tex

**Scope checked:** `references.bib` (41 entries) against `paper_draft.tex` (all `\cite{}` calls), each cited paper's real arXiv record, and the run's own `research_report.md` (prior-art dossier). All arXiv IDs were re-fetched live via `export.arxiv.org/api/query`; several additional real papers were located via web search.

## Headline finding

**Almost none of the bib entries are outright fabricated — nearly every one is a real paper with a correct title/author/arXiv-ID. The paper's actual defect is systematic *citation-key swapping*: specific numeric/descriptive claims in the Introduction and Related Work are attached to the wrong `\cite{}` key, so the reader is pointed at a real but unrelated paper for each claim, while the paper that *actually* made the claim is either cited elsewhere for something else, or is missing from the bibliography entirely.** This affects essentially every sentence in the "Activation-based safety readouts," "Recognition versus execution," and "Depth profiles" paragraphs of Related Work (Sec. 2, lines 28–35).

## 1. Per-entry verification (title/author/id checks, incl. the named suspects)

Verified live against arXiv/IEEE:

| Key | Bib title/authors/id | Real record | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Arditi2024** | Arditi, Obeso, Syed, Paleka, **Nina Rimsky**, Gurnee, Nanda; NeurIPS 2024; 2406.11717 | arxiv.org/abs/2406.11717 → same title, authors are Arditi, Obeso, Syed, Paleka, **Nina Panickssery**, Gurnee, Nanda | **Wrong author name.** "Rimsky" should be "Panickssery." Everything else (title, venue, id) correct. |
| **Yang2026** | "Beyond a Single Direction: Chain-of-Thought Disrupts Simple Steering of Refusal," Kia-Jüng Yang et al., 2605.26772 | arxiv.org/abs/2605.26772 → confirmed, exact title/author match, real paper about LRM chain-of-thought disrupting refusal steering | **Bib entry itself is real and correct** — but see §2: in-text it is used to cite AMS, a completely different paper. |
| **Winninger2026** | "Fast Multi-dimensional Refusal Subspaces via RFM-AGOP," Thomas Winninger, 2607.02396 | arxiv.org/abs/2607.02396 → confirmed exact match | **Bib entry correct** — but used in-text to cite N-GLARE (see §2). |
| **LlorenteSaguer2026a** | "Harmful Intent as a Geometrically Recoverable Feature of LLM Residual Streams," 2604.18901 | arxiv.org/abs/2604.18901 → confirmed exact title/author | Correct. |
| **LlorenteSaguer2026b** | "The Geometry of Harmful Intent: Training-Free Anomaly Detection via Angular Deviation…," 2603.27412 | arxiv.org/abs/2603.27412 → confirmed exact title/author | Correct. |
| **Orgad2026** | "…Distinct Mechanism, Shared Across Harm Types," Orgad, Wei, K. Zheng, Wattenberg, Henderson, Goldfarb-Tarrant, Belinkov | Matches arxiv.org/abs/2604.09544 exactly (K. Zheng = Kaden Zheng) | Correct content, though bib entry is missing the arXiv id/volume. |
| **Lin2025** | "N-GLARE…," Zheyu Lin, Jirui Yang, Yupeng Qiu, Yubing Bao, Heng-Qi Guo, Yao Guan, 2511.14195 | arxiv.org/abs/2511.14195 → real title matches; real author list is Zheyu Lin, Jirui Yang, **Yukui** Qiu, **Hengqi** Guo, Yubing Bao, Yao Guan (minor name/order drift, not fabrication) | Bib entry is genuinely N-GLARE — but see §2, it is *never cited for N-GLARE content in the text*. |
| **Marshall2024** | "Refusal in LLMs is an Affine Function," Marshall, Scherlis, Belrose, 2411.09003 | arxiv.org/abs/2411.09003 → confirmed real title/authors; abstract is about **affine concept editing (ACE)** for steering, nothing about abliteration survival | Bib entry itself correct, but the claim attached to it in-text ("harm recognition geometry survives abliteration in two model families," lines 5 & 32) **does not exist in this paper** — see §2. |
| **Mitra2026** | "Closing the Activation-Cone Blind Spot…," Subhadip Mitra, 2606.29441 | arxiv.org/abs/2606.29441 → confirmed; abstract: prompt-time defenses "structurally blind to prefilling attacks," first-generated-token probing | Correct, and correctly cited. |
| **Yamaguchi2025** | "Where Do Reasoning Models Refuse?," Yamaguchi, Etheridge, Arditi | Real paper confirmed at **arxiv.org/abs/2507.03167** (bib entry omits the id/volume) — but its actual content is about *where in a chain-of-thought a reasoning model's refusal decision occurs*, nothing about Qwen-2.5/LLaMA-3.1 divergence depth | Bib entry roughly right (missing id), but see §2 — the claim cited to it is not in the paper. |
| **Schirmer2026** | "Online Safety Monitoring for LLMs," Schirmer, Jazbec, Timans, Naesseth, Waldron, Nalisnick, 2607.02510 | arxiv.org/abs/2607.02510 → confirmed; abstract is about runtime monitoring/alarms for unsafe deployment-time outputs | Bib entry correct — but see §2, the "staging" claim cited to it is from a different, uncited paper. |
| **Galeone2026** | "Perfect Detection, Failed Control…," C. Galeone, A. Ettorre, M. Park, G. Ettorre, D. Ligorio, 2606.24952 | arxiv.org/abs/2606.24952 → confirmed | Correct entry, but see §2 for a mischaracterization of its finding. |
| **Hurtado2026** | "Has This Checkpoint Been Abliterated?…," Gabriel Hurtado, 2607.01854 | Confirmed: "0.95 AUROC across 273 checkpoints," reference-anchored (needs the parent) | Correct, and correctly cited ("AUROC 0.95," "requires access to the unmodified parent"). |
| **Rottger2023** (XSTest), **Zou2023** (RepE), **Cui2024** (OR-Bench), **Wei2023**, **Li2023**, **Yuan2024** | — | All confirmed against arXiv with exact title/author matches (2308.01263, 2310.01405, 2405.20947, 2307.02483, 2306.03341, 2407.09121) | Correct. |
| **Jiang2026** | "When Behavioral Safety Evaluation Fails: A Representation-Level Perspective," Jiang, Gjølbye, Zhang, Koyejo, 2606.08044 | Confirmed real, exact match; about "dissociated" models defeating static probes | Bib entry correct — but see §2, misused for "Behavioural IRT" in-text. |
| **Wu2026 / Huang2026** | Wu2026="Knowing without Acting…" (Recognition/Execution axes), 2603.05773; Huang2026="RAS…," 2606.25750 | Both confirmed real and correctly matched to their bib entries | Both correct entries — but see §2, badly mismatched to in-text claims. |
| **Messenger2026** | "Detecting Safety Training Modification in Language Models via Activation Analysis," Glen Messenger, IEEE Access vol 14, pp 91723–91737 | Confirmed: this is the real title/author of **AMS**, the Google paper also on arXiv as **2608.05578** ("AMS: Activation-based Model Scanner"), IEEE Access publication confirmed via web search (research.google, ieeexplore.ieee.org/…/11568484) | **This is the correct, real AMS citation** — but is never used for AMS content in-text (see §2). |

## 2. The core problem: claims attached to the wrong `\cite{}` key

Cross-checking every specific numeric/descriptive claim against the paper it's cited to (via live abstracts and the dossier's verbatim quotes) turns up a consistent pattern — every one of the following is a *real, uncontested claim from a real paper*, but attached to the wrong bib key. The paper the claim actually belongs to is either present in the bib under a different key, or absent from the bib entirely:

1. **AMS's numbers (16 contrastive pairs, 40–80% depth, 14 configs/4 families, LOO 71% accuracy) — cited as `\cite{Yang2026}`** (Sec 1 & Sec 2, and again at line 233 "AMS achieves leave-one-out threshold accuracy of 71%… \cite{Yang2026}"). Yang2026 is actually "Beyond a Single Direction: Chain-of-Thought Disrupts Simple Steering of Refusal" (2605.26772) — a reasoning-model CoT paper with nothing to do with AMS. **The correct key for these numbers is already in the bib: `Messenger2026`** (real title "Detecting Safety Training Modification in Language Models via Activation Analysis," arXiv 2608.05578 / IEEE Access), which the paper instead misuses for a different claim (below).

2. **N-GLARE's claim ("Jensen–Shannon divergence across layer groups," "high coupling with unsafe and refusal rates") — cited as `\cite{Winninger2026}`** (Sec 1 line 5, Sec 2 line 29). Winninger2026 is actually "Fast Multi-dimensional Refusal Subspaces via RFM-AGOP" (2607.02396) — unrelated. **The correct key is already in the bib: `Lin2025`**, whose real title *is* N-GLARE, but which is never cited for N-GLARE content anywhere in the paper.

3. **RAS's claim ("reference-anchored score calibrated within families… requires a reference model per family") — cited as `\cite{Wu2026}`** (Sec 2 line 29). Wu2026 is actually "Knowing without Acting: The Disentangled Geometry of Safety Mechanisms" (2603.05773), the paper that owns the "Recognition Axis / Execution Axis" terminology — unrelated to RAS. **The correct key is already in the bib: `Huang2026`** ("RAS: Measuring LLM Safety Through Refusal Alignment," 2606.25750) — which is **never cited anywhere in the paper** (it's one of the 22 dead bib entries).

4. **"Lin et al. formalised this as two subspaces, a Recognition Axis and an Execution Axis" — cited as `\cite{Lin2025}`** (Sec 2 line 32; also line 5). This claim actually belongs to `Wu2026` (Jinman Wu, Yi Xie, **Shen Lin**, Shiqian Zhao, Xiaofeng Chen — note the third author is named Lin, likely the source of the mix-up with N-GLARE's first author Zheyu Lin). Confirmed via live fetch of 2603.05773: "Safety alignment is often conceptualized as a monolithic process wherein harmfulness detection automatically triggers refusal. However, the persistence of jailbreak attacks suggests a fundamental mechanistic decoupling" — this is the Recognition/Execution paper, not N-GLARE.

5. **"Aligned Probing correlates per-layer probe accuracy with output toxicity across 20+ models" — cited as `\cite{Messenger2026}`** (Sec 2 line 29). Messenger2026 is actually the AMS paper (see #1 above), which has nothing to do with toxicity probing. **The real Aligned Probing paper (Waldis, Gautam, Lauscher, Klakow, Gurevych, TACL 2026, aclanthology.org/2026.tacl-1.14.pdf) is absent from the bibliography entirely.**

6. **"Behavioural IRT estimates a latent safety trait from roughly ten adaptively chosen items across 192 models" — cited as `\cite{Jiang2026}`** (Sec 2 line 29). Jiang2026 is actually "When Behavioral Safety Evaluation Fails: A Representation-Level Perspective" (2606.08044, confirmed) — about adversarially "dissociated" models defeating static probes, not IRT. **The real IRT paper ("Item Response Theory for AI Safety," arXiv 2608.05086, "roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice… 192 language models") is absent from the bibliography.**

7. **"Yamaguchi et al. showed that Qwen-2.5 diverges… at approximately 40% depth… more robust to linear steering than LLaMA-3.1, which processes them identically for 95% of layers" — cited as `\cite{Yamaguchi2025}`** (Sec 2 line 35). The real Yamaguchi et al. paper (2507.03167, confirmed via WebSearch, authors Yamaguchi/Etheridge/Arditi) is about *where in a chain-of-thought* a reasoning model decides to refuse — it says nothing about Qwen-vs-LLaMA depth-of-divergence. **The claim actually belongs to a completely different, uncited paper: "The Geometry of Refusal: Linear Instability in Safety-Aligned LLMs" by Shivam Ratnakar & Kartikeya Vats (arXiv 2606.22686), confirmed live: "Models like Llama-3.1 process harmful and safe queries identically for 95% of their layers, diverging only at the final output head"** — this paper is absent from the bibliography.

8. **"Schirmer et al. identified staging of harmfulness detection, aggregation along a refusal direction, and late-layer expression" — cited as `\cite{Schirmer2026}`** (Sec 2 line 34). The real Schirmer et al. paper (2607.02510, confirmed) is about online/runtime safety monitoring and alarms — nothing about layer-staging. **The staging claim belongs to a different, uncited paper: "Refusal Lives Downstream of Persona in Chat Models" by Viola Zhong & Qirui Li (arXiv 2606.26161), confirmed: "This mediation unfolds across layers in three stages: input-side harmfulness detection, aggregation along the refusal direction, and late-layer expression."**

9. **"Marshall showed that geometry associated with harm recognition survives abliteration in two model families" — cited as `\cite{Marshall2024}`** (Sec 2 line 32; also bundled at line 5 with LlorenteSaguer2026a). Confirmed via live fetch (2411.09003): Marshall/Scherlis/Belrose's real paper is "Refusal in LLMs is an Affine Function," about affine concept editing (ACE) for steering — it makes no claim about geometry surviving abliteration across model families. **This is exactly the fabricated attribution the task flagged.** The real source of "geometry survives refusal ablation in both tested model families" is `LlorenteSaguer2026b` (2603.27412, per the dossier's own verbatim quote) — which is already co-cited in the same sentence, making the Marshall2024 addition a spurious extra citation for a claim its paper doesn't make.

## 3. The two specific Sec 2/Sec 5.1 sentences flagged in the task

- **"Public abliteration toolkits already compute pairwise cosine similarities and cumulative angular drift across layers `\cite{Arditi2024}`"** (line 35). This **is a miscitation**. Arditi et al.'s NeurIPS 2024 paper introduces the abliteration method itself; it does not build or describe "public toolkits" with cosine-similarity/angular-drift dashboards. Per the run's own dossier (source [27]), that functionality belongs to a third-party, non-peer-reviewed community tool, the **OBLITERATUS** GitHub repo, whose `analysis/cross_layer.py` computes exactly "pairwise cosine similarities between refusal directions across all layers" and "cumulative angular drift per layer" — this tool is not in the bibliography at all, and Arditi2024 is cited here for something its authors never built or claimed.

- **"consecutive-layer cosines can be as low as 0.016 in deep layers" (Sec 5.1, Discussion, line 227)** — has **no citation whatsoever** in the text, and I could not locate this specific figure ("0.016") in any of the papers checked, the OBLITERATUS repo excerpts in the dossier, or via web search. This appears to be an **unsourced, unverifiable number** inserted into the Discussion without support.

- **"This finding is consistent with Galeone et al.'s report that weight cosines between layer-wise directions are low and do not predict steerability `\cite{Galeone2026}`"** (line 227) — I fetched the actual paper (arxiv.org/abs/2606.24952) and confirmed its cosine finding is **not** about layer-to-layer rotation within a model. Its actual measurement is the cosine between the *detection direction* and the *steering/control direction* (two different directions at the same site), found to be stable — "cos stays in [0.12, 0.20], identical before and after instruction tuning" (i.e., stable across training checkpoints, not across depth). The paper's characterization ("weight cosine between layer-wise directions… not predictive of steerability") **conflates two different geometric comparisons** (cross-layer rotation vs. detection-vs-control-direction stability across training) and mischaracterizes what Galeone2026 actually measured.

## 4. Bib coverage counts

- 41 total `.bib` entries; **19 unique keys actually `\cite`'d**; **0 cited keys missing from the bib** (no undefined references — LaTeX would compile without `??`).

Most of the previous round's must-fix items are also fixed. It still has to block, for these reasons:

1. **The held-out panel is described as the wrong models.** The paper says Qwen3, SmolLM3, Phi-4-mini and Granite. The panel that actually ran is Vikhr-Llama, Falcon3, AMD-OLMo, Llama-3.2 and LFM2. 3. **The held-out table shows 2 of the 30+ registered readouts.** It leaves out B7 (+0.80, which traces to an architecture quirk in AMD-OLMo) and several readouts that pass the critical value with the opposite sign to their pre-registration. 4. **The "layer-specific direction" claim holds only on in-distribution prompts.** On the held-out prompts the test is undefined, or the depth trend is positive in three of the five models.
5. **The Lane B lineages are mislabelled.** L1 is the Qwen3-4B Base model, not Instruct, so part of the safety-vs-non-safety comparison is wrong.

The review recommends a new headline. Held-out request-axis d drops in all 8 abliterated pairs and doesn't move on either no-change control, while the logit baseline moves on both. ## 2. The core problem: claims attached to the wrong `\cite{}` key

Cross-checking every specific numeric/descriptive claim against the paper it's cited to (via live abstracts and the dossier's verbatim quotes) turns up a consistent pattern — every one of the following is a *real, uncontested claim from a real paper*, but attached to the wrong bib key. The paper the claim actually belongs to is either present in the bib under a different key, or absent from the bib entirely:

1. **AMS's numbers (16 contrastive pairs, 40–80% depth, 14 configs/4 families, LOO 71% accuracy) — cited as `\cite{Yang2026}`** (Sec 1 &amp; Sec 2, and again at line 233 "AMS achieves leave-one-out threshold accuracy of 71%… \cite{Yang2026}"). Yang2026 is actually "Beyond a Single Direction: Chain-of-Thought Disrupts Simple Steering of Refusal" (2605.26772) — a reasoning-model CoT paper with nothing to do with AMS. **The correct key for these numbers is already in the bib: `Messenger2026`** (real title "Detecting Safety Training Modification in Language Models via Activation Analysis," arXiv 2608.05578 / IEEE Access), which the paper instead misuses for a different claim (below).

2. **N-GLARE's claim ("Jensen–Shannon divergence across layer groups," "high coupling with unsafe and refusal rates") — cited as `\cite{Winninger2026}`** (Sec 1 line 5, Sec 2 line 29). Winninger2026 is actually "Fast Multi-dimensional Refusal Subspaces via RFM-AGOP" (2607.02396) — unrelated. **The correct key is already in the bib: `Lin2025`**, whose real title *is* N-GLARE, but which is never cited for N-GLARE content anywhere in the paper.

3. **RAS's claim ("reference-anchored score calibrated within families… requires a reference model per family") — cited as `\cite{Wu2026}`** (Sec 2 line 29). Wu2026 is actually "Knowing without Acting: The Disentangled Geometry of Safety Mechanisms" (2603.05773), the paper that owns the "Recognition Axis / Execution Axis" terminology — unrelated to RAS. **The correct key is already in the bib: `Huang2026`** ("RAS: Measuring LLM Safety Through Refusal Alignment," 2606.25750) — which is **never cited anywhere in the paper** (it's one of the 22 dead bib entries).

4. **"Lin et al. formalised this as two subspaces, a Recognition Axis and an Execution Axis" — cited as `\cite{Lin2025}`** (Sec 2 line 32; also line 5). This claim actually belongs to `Wu2026` (Jinman Wu, Yi Xie, **Shen Lin**, Shiqian Zhao, Xiaofeng Chen — note the third author is named Lin, likely the source of the mix-up with N-GLARE's first author Zheyu Lin). Confirmed via live fetch of 2603.05773: "Safety alignment is often conceptualized as a monolithic process wherein harmfulness detection automatically triggers refusal. However, the persistence of jailbreak attacks suggests a fundamental mechanistic decoupling" — this is the Recognition/Execution paper, not N-GLARE.

5. **"Aligned Probing correlates per-layer probe accuracy with output toxicity across 20+ models" — cited as `\cite{Messenger2026}`** (Sec 2 line 29). Messenger2026 is actually the AMS paper (see #1 above), which has nothing to do with toxicity probing. **The real Aligned Probing paper (Waldis, Gautam, Lauscher, Klakow, Gurevych, TACL 2026, aclanthology.org/2026.tacl-1.14.pdf) is absent from the bibliography entirely.**

6. **"Behavioural IRT estimates a latent safety trait from roughly ten adaptively chosen items across 192 models" — cited as `\cite{Jiang2026}`** (Sec 2 line 29). Jiang2026 is actually "When Behavioral Safety Evaluation Fails: A Representation-Level Perspective" (2606.08044, confirmed) — about adversarially "dissociated" models defeating static probes, not IRT. **The real IRT paper ("Item Response Theory for AI Safety," arXiv 2608.05086, "roughly ten adaptively chosen items suffice… 192 language models") is absent from the bibliography.**

7. **"Yamaguchi et al. showed that Qwen-2.5 diverges… at approximately 40% depth… more robust to linear steering than LLaMA-3.1, which processes them identically for 95% of layers" — cited as `\cite{Yamaguchi2025}`** (Sec 2 line 35). The real Yamaguchi et al. paper (2507.03167, confirmed via WebSearch, authors Yamaguchi/Etheridge/Arditi) is about *where in a chain-of-thought* a reasoning model decides to refuse — it says nothing about Qwen-vs-LLaMA depth-of-divergence. **The claim actually belongs to a completely different, uncited paper: "The Geometry of Refusal: Linear Instability in Safety-Aligned LLMs" by Shivam Ratnakar &amp; Kartikeya Vats (arXiv 2606.22686), confirmed live: "Models like Llama-3.1 process harmful and safe queries identically for 95% of their layers, diverging only at the final output head"** — this paper is absent from the bibliography.

8. **"Schirmer et al. identified staging of harmfulness detection, aggregation along a refusal direction, and late-layer expression" — cited as `\cite{Schirmer2026}`** (Sec 2 line 34). The real Schirmer et al. paper (2607.02510, confirmed) is about online/runtime safety monitoring and alarms — nothing about layer-staging. **The staging claim belongs to a different, uncited paper: "Refusal Lives Downstream of Persona in Chat Models" by Viola Zhong &amp; Qirui Li (arXiv 2606.26161), confirmed: "This mediation unfolds across layers in three stages: input-side harmfulness detection, aggregation along the refusal direction, and late-layer expression."**

9. **"Marshall showed that geometry associated with harm recognition survives abliteration in two model families" — cited as `\cite{Marshall2024}`** (Sec 2 line 32; also bundled at line 5 with LlorenteSaguer2026a). Confirmed via live fetch (2411.09003): Marshall/Scherlis/Belrose's real paper is "Refusal in LLMs is an Affine Function," about affine concept editing (ACE) for steering — it makes no claim about geometry surviving abliteration across model families. **This is exactly the fabricated attribution the task flagged.** The real source of "geometry survives refusal ablation in both tested model families" is `LlorenteSaguer2026b` (2603.27412, per the dossier's own verbatim quote) — which is already co-cited in the same sentence, making the Marshall2024 addition a spurious extra citation for a claim its paper doesn't make.

## 3. The two specific Sec 2/Sec 5.1 sentences flagged in the task

- **"Public abliteration toolkits already compute pairwise cosine similarities and cumulative angular drift across layers `\cite{Arditi2024}`"** (line 35). This **is a miscitation**. Arditi et al.'s NeurIPS 2024 paper introduces the abliteration method itself; it does not build or describe "public toolkits" with cosine-similarity/angular-drift dashboards. Per the run's own dossier (source [27]), that functionality belongs to a third-party, non-peer-reviewed community tool, the **OBLITERATUS** GitHub repo, whose `analysis/cross_layer.py` computes exactly "pairwise cosine similarities between refusal directions across all layers" and "cumulative angular drift per layer" — this tool is not in the bibliography at all, and Arditi2024 is cited here for something its authors never built or claimed.

- **"consecutive-layer cosines can be as low as 0.016 in deep layers" (Sec 5.1, Discussion, line 227)** — has **no citation whatsoever** in the text, and I could not locate this specific figure ("0.016") in any of the papers checked, the OBLITERATUS repo excerpts in the dossier, or via web search. This appears to be an **unsourced, unverifiable number** inserted into the Discussion without support.

- **"This finding is consistent with Galeone et al.'s report that weight cosines between layer-wise directions are low and do not predict steerability `\cite{Galeone2026}`"** (line 227) — I fetched the actual paper (arxiv.org/abs/2606.24952) and confirmed its cosine finding is **not** about layer-to-layer rotation within a model. Its actual measurement is the cosine between the *detection direction* and the *steering/control direction* (two different directions at the same site), found to be stable — "cos stays in [0.12, 0.20], identical before and after instruction tuning" (i.e., stable across training checkpoints, not across depth). The paper's characterization ("weight cosine between layer-wise directions… not predictive of steerability") **conflates two different geometric comparisons** (cross-layer rotation vs. detection-vs-control-direction stability across training) and mischaracterizes what Galeone2026 actually measured.
